```python
import sys
from collections import defaultdict

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    points = []
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().split()
        x, y, d = int(line[0]), int(line[1]), line[2]
        points.append((x, y, d))
    
    # If only one point, area is 0
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Function to calculate area at time t
    def area_at_time(t):
        xs = []
        ys = []
        for x, y, d in points:
            if d == 'R':
                xs.append(x + t)
            elif d == 'L':
                xs.append(x - t)
            elif d == 'U':
                ys.append(y + t)
            elif d == 'D':
                ys.append(y - t)
            else:
                # This shouldn't happen
                pass
        
        x_min, x_max = min(xs), max(xs) if xs else 0
        y_min, y_max = min(ys), max(ys) if ys else 0
        
        # If no points in x or y, set to 0
        if not xs:
            x_min, x_max = 0, 0
        if not ys:
            y_min, y_max = 0, 0
            
        return (x_max - x_min) * (y_max - y_min)
    
    # Check at t = 0
    min_area = area_at_time(0)
    
    # Find all meeting times between pairs of points
    # For each pair, solve when their positions become equal
    meeting_times = set()
    
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, d1 = points[i]
            x2, y2, d2 = points[j]
            
            # Check if they can meet at time t
            # We need to solve for t such that positions are equal
            
            # Case 1: Both move horizontally (R,L)
            if d1 in 'RL' and d2 in 'RL':
                if d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'R':  # Both R
                    # x1 + t = x2 + t → x1 = x2
                    if x1 == x2:
                        # They have same x, different y initially
                        # To meet, y1 must equal y2 at some time
                        # If they have same x, they meet only if y1 = y2
                        # Otherwise, they never meet
                        if y1 == y2:
                            # They meet at any t, so t can be any value
                            # We want to check if they can meet, which they can
                            # Actually, with same x and same y, they already meet
                            pass
                elif d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'L':
                    # x1 + t = x2 - t → 2t = x2 - x1 → t = (x2 - x1)/2
                    if x1 <= x2:  # t >= 0
                        t = (x2 - x1) / 2.0
                        meeting_times.add(t)
                elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'R':
                    # x1 - t = x2 + t → -2t = x2 - x1 → t = (x1 - x2)/2
                    if x1 >= x2:  # t >= 0  
                        t = (x1 - x2) / 2.0
                        meeting_times.add(t)
                elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'L':  # Both L
                    # x1 - t = x2 - t → x1 = x2
                    if x1 == x2:
                        # They have same x, different y
                        if y1 == y2:
                            pass
            # Case 2: Both move vertically (U,D)
            elif d1 in 'UD' and d2 in 'UD':
                if d1 == 'U' and d2 == 'U':
                    if y1 == y2:
                        pass
                elif d1 == 'U' and d2 == 'D':
                    # y1 + t = y2 - t → 2t = y2 - y1 → t = (y2 - y1)/2
                    if y1 <= y2:
                        t = (y2 - y1) / 2.0
                        meeting_times.add(t)
                elif d1 == 'D' and d2 == 'U':
                    # y1 - t = y2 + t → -2t = y2 - y1 → t = (y1 - y2)/2
                    if y1 >= y2:
                        t = (y1 - y2) / 2.0
                        meeting_times.add(t)
                elif d1 == 'D' and d2 == 'D':
                    if y1 == y2:
                        pass
            # Case 3: One horizontal, one vertical
            elif d1 in 'RL' and d2 in 'UD':
                # They can meet only if their initial positions allow it
                # For R and U: x1 + t = x2, y1 + t = y2 + t → y1 = y2
                if d1 in 'RL' and d2 in 'UD':
                    if d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'U':
                        # x1 + t = x2, y1 + t = y2 + t → y1 = y2
                        if y1 == y2:
                            t = x2 - x1
                            if t >= 0:
                                meeting_times.add(t)
                    elif d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'D':
                        # x1 + t = x2, y1 + t = y2 - t → y1 + t = y2 - t → 2t = y2 - y1 → t = (y2 - y1)/2
                        # x1 + t = x2 → t = x2 - x1
                        # So we need t = x2 - x1 and t = (y2 - y1)/2
                        # They meet only if x2 - x1 = (y2 - y1)/2
                        if y2 - y1 == 2 * (x2 - x1):
                            t = x2 - x1
                            if t >= 0:
                                meeting_times.add(t)
                    elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'U':
                        # x1 - t = x2, y1 + t = y2 + t → y1 = y2
                        if y1 == y2:
                            t = x2 - x1
                            if t >= 0:  # x1 - t = x2 → t = x1 - x2
                                if t >= 0:

                        # x1 - t = x2 → t = x1 - x2
                        # So need x1 - x2 = (y2 - y1)/2
                        if y2 - y1 == 2 * (x1 - x2):
                            t = x1 - x2
                            if t >= 0:
                                meeting_times.add(t)
    
    # Add time 0 check (already did)
    # Check all meeting times
    for t in meeting_times:
        area = area_at_time(t)
        if area < min_area:
            min_area = area
    
    # Also check if we can make all points have same x-coordinate or same y-coordinate
    # This is complex, but if area is 0, that's the best
    
    # The answer could be 0 if we can make all points meet at a point
    # Let's check if there exists a time t >= 0 where all points can be made to meet
    # For that, we need all points to have the same position (x, y)
    
    # Instead, let me check if area can be 0
    # Area is 0 when x_min = x_max or y_min = y_max
    
    # But given the complexity, let me simplify - the answer in the sample is 0
    # And the problem is asking for minimum possible area
    
    # Actually, I think for most cases, we need to check the critical times
    
    print(min_area)
```

Wait, this is getting too complicated. Let me reconsider with a simpler approach.

After reviewing, I realize that we need to check when the bounding box collapses to zero area, which happens when points meet or align.

The key insight is that the minimum area is 0 if we can make all points meet at a common point, or if we can align all points to have the same x or same y coordinate.

For the problem constraints, we need to check all critical times when points meet. But the simple approach might miss some cases.

Actually, looking at the sample: two points (0,3)D and (3,0)L meet at origin at t=3.

For point 1 at (0,3)D: moves down, so at time t: (0, 3-t)
For point 2 at (3,0)L: moves left, so at time t: (3-t, 0)

They meet when 3-t = 0 and 3-t = 0, so t=3.

After t=3, both are at (0,0), area = 0.

So I need to find all meeting times between pairs of points, and evaluate the area at those times.

```python
import sys

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    points = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, d = input().split()
        points.append((int(x), int(y), d))
    
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Function to calculate the area at time t
    def area_at_time(t):
        x_coords = []
        y_coords = []
        
        for x, y, d in points:
            if d == 'R':
                x_coords.append(x + t)
            elif d == 'L':
                x_coords.append(x - t)
            elif d == 'U':
                y_coords.append(y + t)
            elif d == 'D':
                y_coords.append(y - t)
        
        if not x_coords:
            x_min, x_max = 0, 0
        else:
            x_min, x_max = min(x_coords), max(x_coords)
            
        if not y_coords:
            y_min, y_max = 0, 0
        else:
            y_min, y_max = min(y_coords), max(y_coords)
            
        return (x_max - x_min) * (y_max - y_min)
    
    # The minimum area will be at critical times
    # Critical times include t=0 and times when any two points meet
    critical_times = [0.0]
    
    # Check all pairs for meeting times
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, d1 = points[i]
            x2, y2, d2 = points[j]
            
            # Case 1: both horizontal
            if d1 in 'RL' and d2 in 'RL':
                if d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'R':
                    # x1 + t = x2 + t → x1 = x2
                    if x1 == x2:
                        # They have same x, so need y coordinates to meet
                        # They meet only if y1 = y2 or they can meet in y-direction
                        # But since both are R, they only meet if y1 = y2
                        if y1 == y2:
                            # They meet at any time, so add t as a critical time
                            # But we already have t=0, so no need
                            pass
                elif d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'L':
                    # x1 + t = x2 - t → 2t = x2 - x1
                    t_val = (x2 - x1) / 2.0
                    if t_val >= 0:
                        critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'R':
                    # x1 - t = x2 + t → -2t = x2 - x1
                    t_val = (x1 - x2) / 2.0
                    if t_val >= 0:
                        critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'L':
                    # x1 - t = x2 - t → x1 = x2
                    if x1 == x2:
                        if y1 == y2:
                            pass
            # Case 2: both vertical
            elif d1 in 'UD' and d2 in 'UD':
                if d1 == 'U' and d2 == 'U':
                    # y1 + t = y2 + t → y1 = y2
                    if y1 == y2:
                        pass
                elif d1 == 'U' and d2 == 'D':
                    # y1 + t = y2 - t → 2t = y2 - y1
                    t_val = (y2 - y1) / 2.0
                    if t_val >= 0:
                        critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'D' and d2 == 'U':
                    # y1 - t = y2 + t → -2t = y2 - y1
                    t_val = (y1 - y2) / 2.0
                    if t_val >= 0:
                        critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'D' and d2 == 'D':
                    if y1 == y2:
                        pass
            # Case 3: one horizontal, one vertical
            elif d1 in 'RL' and d2 in 'UD':
                # For R and U: 
                # x1 + t = x2, y1 + t = y2 + t → y1 = y2
                # For R and D: 
                # x1 + t = x2, y1 + t = y2 - t → 2t = y2 - y1
                # For L and U:
                # x1 - t = x2, y1 + t = y2 + t → y1 = y2
                # For L and D:
                # x1 - t = x2, y1 + t = y2 - t → 2t = y2 - y1
                
                if d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'U':
                    if y1 == y2:
                        t_val = x2 - x1
                        if t_val >= 0:
                            critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'D':
                    if 2 * (y2 - y1) == 2 * (x2 - x1):
                        t_val = x2 - x1
                        if t_val >= 0:
                            critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'U':
                    if y1 == y2:
                        t_val = x2 - x1  # x1 - t = x2 → t = x1 - x2
                        if t_val >= 0:
                            critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'D':
                    if 2 * (y2 - y1) == 2 * (x1 - x2):
                        t_val = x1 - x2
                        if t_val >= 0:
                            critical_times.append(t_val)
    
    # Evaluate area at all critical times
    min_area = float('inf')
    
    for t in critical_times:
        area = area_at_time(t)
        if area < min_area:
            min_area = area
    
    print(min_area)
```

```python
import sys

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    points = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, d = input().split()
        points.append((int(x), int(y), d))
    
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Function to calculate the area at time t
    def area_at_time(t):
        x_coords = []
        y_coords = []
        
        for x, y, d in points:
            if d == 'R':
                x_coords.append(x + t)
            elif d == 'L':
                x_coords.append(x - t)
            elif d == 'U':
                y_coords.append(y + t)
            elif d == 'D':
                y_coords.append(y - t)
        
        if not x_coords:
            x_min, x_max = 0, 0
        else:
            x_min, x_max = min(x_coords), max(x_coords)
            
        if not y_coords:
            y_min, y_max = 0, 0
        else:
            y_min, y_max = min(y_coords), max(y_coords)
            
        return (x_max - x_min) * (y_max - y_min)
    
    # The minimum area will be at critical times
    critical_times = [0.0]
    
    # Check all pairs for meeting times
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, d1 = points[i]
            x2, y2, d2 = points[j]
            
            # Case 1: both horizontal
            if d1 in 'RL' and d2 in 'RL':
                if d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'R':
                    # Only meet if x1 == x2 and y1 == y2 (already covered)
                    if x1 == x2:
                        # Can meet if y1 == y2
                        if y1 == y2:
                            # They meet at any time, add a critical time
                            pass
                elif d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'L':
                    # x1 + t = x2 - t → 2t = x2 - x1
                    t_val = (x2 - x1) / 2.0
                    if t_val >= 0:
                        critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'R':
                    # x1 - t = x2 + t → -2t = x2 - x1
                    t_val = (x1 - x2) / 2.0
                    if t_val >= 0:
                        critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'L':
                    # Only meet if x1 == x2 and y1 == y2
                    if x1 == x2:
                        if y1 == y2:
                            pass
            # Case 2: both vertical
            elif d1 in 'UD' and d2 in 'UD':
                if d1 == 'U' and d2 == 'U':
                    # Only meet if y1 == y2 and x1 == x2
                    if y1 == y2:
                        pass
                elif d1 == 'U' and d2 == 'D':
                    # y1 + t = y2 - t → 2t = y2 - y1
                    t_val = (y2 - y1) / 2.0
                    if t_val >= 0:
                        critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'D' and d2 == 'U':
                    # y1 - t = y2 + t → -2t = y2 - y1
                    t_val = (y1 - y2) / 2.0
                    if t_val >= 0:
                        critical_times.append(t_val)
                elif d1 == 'D' and d2 == 'D':
                    # Only meet if y1 == y2 and x1 == x2
                    if y1 == y2:
                        pass
            # Case 3: one horizontal, one vertical
            elif d1 in 'RL' and d2 in 'UD':
                # For R and U:
                if d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'U':
                    # x1 + t = x2, and y1 + t = y2 + t → y1 = y2
                    if y1 == y2:
                        t_val = x2 - x1
                        if t_val >= 0:
                            critical_times.append(t_val)
                # For R and D:
                elif d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'D':
                    # x1 + t = x2, and y1 + t = y2 - t → y1 + t = y2 - t → 2t = y2 - y1
                    # x1 + t = x2 → t = x2 - x1
                    # So we need x2 - x1 = (y2 - y1)/2
                    if 2 * (x2 - x1) == (y2 - y1):
                        t_val = x2 - x1
                        if t_val >= 0:
                            critical_times.append(t_val)
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

There are N points in a two-dimensional plane. The initial coordinates of the i-th point are (x_i, y_i). Now, each point starts moving at a speed of 1 per second, in a direction parallel to the x- or y- axis. You are given a character d_i that represents the specific direction in which the i-th point moves, as follows:
 - If d_i = R, the i-th point moves in the positive x direction;
 - If d_i = L, the i-th point moves in the negative x direction;
 - If d_i = U, the i-th point moves in the positive y direction;
 - If d_i = D, the i-th point moves in the negative y direction.
You can stop all the points at some moment of your choice after they start moving (including the moment they start moving).
Then, let x_{max} and x_{min} be the maximum and minimum among the x-coordinates of the N points, respectively. Similarly, let y_{max} and y_{min} be the maximum and minimum among the y-coordinates of the N points, respectively.
Find the minimum possible value of (x_{max} - x_{min}) \times (y_{max} - y_{min}) and print it.

-----Constraints-----
 - 1 \leq N \leq 10^5
 - -10^8 \leq x_i,\ y_i \leq 10^8
 - x_i and y_i are integers.
 - d_i is R, L, U, or D.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
x_1 y_1 d_1
x_2 y_2 d_2
.
.
.
x_N y_N d_N

-----Output-----
Print the minimum possible value of (x_{max} - x_{min}) \times (y_{max} - y_{min}).
The output will be considered correct when its absolute or relative error from the judge's output is at most 10^{-9}.

-----Sample Input-----
2
0 3 D
3 0 L

-----Sample Output-----
0

After three seconds, the two points will meet at the origin. The value in question will be 0 at that moment.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N points moving in 2D plane at speed 1 unit/second in one of four directions (R, L, U, D)
2. We can stop all points at any time t ≥ 0
3. We want to minimize the area of the bounding rectangle formed by the points at time t
4. The area is (x_max - x_min) × (y_max - y_min)

## Key Insights

1. At time t, a point's position changes based on its direction:
   - R: (x + t, y)
   - L: (x - t, y)
   - U: (x, y + t)
   - D: (x, y - t)

2. The bounding rectangle area depends on when we stop the points.

3. The key observation is that the optimal stopping time will be when:
   - Two points meet (x-coordinates become equal or y-coordinates become equal)
   - Or when the bounding box collapses to minimum area

4. Since we want to minimize the area, we should consider times when either:
   - Two points with opposite x-directions meet (x-coordinates become equal)
   - Two points with opposite y-directions meet (y-coordinates become equal)
   - Or the configuration results in zero area (one dimension collapses)

5. We only need to check discrete times when the configuration changes significantly, specifically when points meet or when the bounding box collapses.

## Strategy

1. The minimum area could be 0 (when points all meet at same point)
2. Otherwise, we need to find when the bounding rectangle is minimized.

Let me think about when the area can be minimized:
- If points can meet at the same point, area = 0
- Otherwise, we need to check critical times when x-coordinates or y-coordinates align

The critical times are when:
- Two points with opposite x-directions meet
- Two points with opposite y-directions meet

For each pair of points with opposite x-directions (R and L), the time when they meet is:
- R at (x1 + t, y1)
- L at (x2 - t, y2)
- They meet when x1 + t = x2 - t, so t = (x2 - x1) / 2 (if x2 > x1)

For each pair of points with opposite y-directions (U and D):
- U at (x1, y1 + t)
- D at (x2, y2 - t)
- They meet when y1 + t = y2 - t, so t = (y2 - y1) / 2 (if y2 > y1)

Also check t = 0 (initial positions).

The answer is the minimum area among all these critical times.

## Implementation

```python
import sys

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    points = []
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().split()
        x, y = int(line[0]), int(line[1])
        d = line[2]
        points.append((x, y, d))
    
    if n <= 1:
        # If only one point, area is 0 (single point has no width/height)
        return 0
    
    # Critical times to check
    critical_times = [0.0]  # t = 0 initially
    
    # Check times when R and L points meet (x-coordinates equal)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, d1 = points[i]
            x2, y2, d2 = points[j]
            
            if d1 == 'R' and d2 == 'L':
                # R at (x1 + t, y1), L at (x2 - t, y2)
                # They meet when x1 + t = x2 - t => t = (x2 - x1) / 2
                if x2 > x1:
                    t = (x2 - x1) / 2.0
                    critical_times.append(t)
            elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'R':
                # Same as above
                if x1 > x2:
                    t = (x1 - x2) / 2.0
                    critical_times.append(t)
    
    # Check times when U and D points meet (y-coordinates equal)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, d1 = points[i]
            x2, y2, d2 = points[j]
            
            if d1 == 'U' and d2 == 'D':
                # U at (x1, y1 + t), D at (x2, y2 - t)
                # They meet when y1 + t = y2 - t => t = (y2 - y1) / 2
                if y2 > y1:
                    t = (y2 - y1) / 2.0
                    critical_times.append(t)
            elif d1 == 'D' and d2 == 'U':
                # Same as above
                if y1 > y2:
                    t = (y1 - y2) / 2.0
                    critical_times.append(t)
    
    # Also check if we can make area 0 by having all points meet at same point
    # But since we only care about critical times, we'll check all times
    
    min_area = float('inf')
    
    # Check all critical times
    for t in critical_times:
        # Calculate positions at time t
        x_coords = []
        y_coords = []
        
        for x, y, d in points:
            if d == 'R':
                new_x = x + t
                new_y = y
            elif d == 'L':
                new_x = x - t
                new_y = y
            elif d == 'U':
                new_x = x
                new_y = y + t
            elif d == 'D':
                new_x = x
                new_y = y - t
            
            x_coords.append(new_x)
            y_coords.append(new_y)
        
        x_min, x_max = min(x_coords), max(x_coords)
        y_min, y_max = min(y_coords), max(y_coords)
        
        area = (x_max - x_min) * (y_max - y_min)
        min_area = min(min_area, area)
    
    return min_area

print(solve())
```

Wait, I think I'm overcomplicating this. Let me reconsider.

The key insight is that the area function is piecewise linear and the minima occur at critical points where either x-coordinates align or y-coordinates align. The critical times are when two points meet, specifically:

1. When two points moving horizontally in opposite directions meet
2. When two points moving vertically in opposite directions meet
3. When the bounding rectangle collapses to 0

But actually, the answer might be 0 in some cases, like the sample where two points meet at origin after 3 seconds.

Let me verify with the sample:
- Point 1: (0,3) moving D → at time t: (0, 3-t)
- Point 2: (3,0) moving L → at time t: (3-t, 0)

They meet when 0 = 3-t and 3-t = 0, so t=3. At t=3, both are at (0,0), so area = 0.

The critical time is when x-coordinates are equal or y-coordinates are equal.

For the x-coordinates: point 1 has x=0, point 2 has x=3-t. They're equal when 0 = 3-t → t=3.
For the y-coordinates: point 1 has y=3-t, point 2 has y=0. They're equal when 3-t = 0 → t=3.

So the critical time is t=3, which gives area 0.

```python

        elif d1 == 'L' and d2 == 'R':
            # Same as above
            if x1 > x2:
                t = (x1 - x2) / 2.0
                critical_times.append(t)

# Check when opposite y-directions meet (U and D)
for i in range(n):
    for j in range(i + 1, n):
        x1, y1, d1 = points[i]
        x2, y2, d2 = points[j]
        
        if d1 == 'U' and d2 == 'D':
            # U: (x1, y1 + t), D: (x2, y2 - t)
            # They meet when y1 + t = y2 - t => t = (y2 - y1) / 2
            if y2 > y1:
                t = (y2 - y1) / 2.0
                critical_times.append(t)
        elif d1 == 'D' and d2 == 'U':
            # Same as above
            if y1 > y2:
                t = (y1 - y2) / 2.0
                critical_times.append(t)

# Also consider t = 0 and other potential cases
# But we already have 0.0 in the list

min_area = float('inf')

for t in critical_times:
    # Calculate positions at time t
    x_coords = []
    y_coords = []
    
    for x, y, d in points:
        if d == 'R':
            new_x = x + t
            new_y = y
        elif d == 'L':
            new_x = x - t
            new_y = y
        elif d == 'U':
            new_x = x
            new_y = y + t
        elif d == 'D':
            new_x = x
            new_y = y - t
        
        x_coords.append(new_x)
        y_coords.append(new_y)
    
    x_min, x_max = min(x_coords), max(x_coords)
    y_min, y_max = min(y_coords), max(y_coords)
    
    area = (x_max - x_min) * (y_max - y_min)
    min_area = min(min_area, area)

print(min_area)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Gerald plays the following game. He has a checkered field of size n × n cells, where m various cells are banned. Before the game, he has to put a few chips on some border (but not corner) board cells. Then for n - 1 minutes, Gerald every minute moves each chip into an adjacent cell. He moves each chip from its original edge to the opposite edge. Gerald loses in this game in each of the three cases:

  * At least one of the chips at least once fell to the banned cell. 
  * At least once two chips were on the same cell. 
  * At least once two chips swapped in a minute (for example, if you stand two chips on two opposite border cells of a row with even length, this situation happens in the middle of the row). 



In that case he loses and earns 0 points. When nothing like that happened, he wins and earns the number of points equal to the number of chips he managed to put on the board. Help Gerald earn the most points.

Input

The first line contains two space-separated integers n and m (2 ≤ n ≤ 1000, 0 ≤ m ≤ 105) — the size of the field and the number of banned cells. Next m lines each contain two space-separated integers. Specifically, the i-th of these lines contains numbers xi and yi (1 ≤ xi, yi ≤ n) — the coordinates of the i-th banned cell. All given cells are distinct.

Consider the field rows numbered from top to bottom from 1 to n, and the columns — from left to right from 1 to n.

Output

Print a single integer — the maximum points Gerald can earn in this game.

Examples

Input

3 1
2 2


Output

0


Input

3 0


Output

1


Input

4 3
3 1
3 2
3 3


Output

1

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an n×n grid with m banned cells
2. We can only place chips on border cells that are NOT corners (so only edge cells that are not at corners)
3. Each chip moves every minute to an adjacent cell (up, down, left, right)
4. We lose if:
   - Any chip reaches a banned cell
   - Two chips are on the same cell at the same time
   - Two chips swap positions (they cross paths)
5. We want to maximize the number of chips we can place

## Key Insights

1. **Border cells**: For an n×n grid, the border cells are:
   - Top row: (1, j) for j = 2 to n-1
   - Bottom row: (n, j) for j = 2 to n-1  
   - Left column: (i, 1) for i = 2 to n-1
   - Right column: (i, n) for i = 2 to n-1

2. **Movement pattern**: Each chip starts at a border position and moves toward the opposite border. For example:
   - A chip at (1, j) moves down
   - A chip at (n, j) moves up  
   - A chip at (i, 1) moves right
   - A chip at (i, n) moves left

3. **Swapping condition**: Two chips swap when they cross paths. For example:
   - A chip from top row moving down and a chip from bottom row moving up will meet in the middle
   - A chip from left column moving right and a chip from right column moving left will meet in the middle

4. **Same cell condition**: Two chips can't occupy the same cell at the same time.

5. **Banned cell condition**: Any chip that reaches a banned cell causes loss.

## Strategy

The key insight is that we can place at most one chip in each "path segment" to avoid swapping and collisions.

Let me think about this more systematically:

- Chips moving in the same direction (all going down, or all going up, or all going right, or all going left) can potentially avoid collisions.
- Chips moving in opposite directions will eventually meet and swap.

However, since chips move simultaneously and we need to avoid any two chips being on the same cell or swapping, we need to carefully select positions.

Actually, let me reconsider: chips moving in opposite directions will definitely cross paths (swap) in the middle. So we can only have at most one chip in each "direction pair".

But looking at the examples:

**Example 1**: n=3, m=1 (banned at (2,2))
- Border non-corner cells: (1,2), (3,2), (2,1), (2,3)
- Any two chips will eventually meet or swap - answer is 0

**Example 2**: n=3, m=0
- Can place at most 1 chip (since any two would either meet or swap) - answer is 1

**Example 3**: n=4, banned at (3,1), (3,2), (3,3)
- Border non-corner cells: (1,2), (1,3), (4,2), (4,3), (2,1), (2,4), (3,1) is banned, (3,4) is banned? (3,4) is on border but column 4, row 3 - (3,4) is border cell
- Wait, (3,1), (3,2), (3,3) are banned, so (3,4) is banned? No, (3,4) has y=4 which is the right border, but x=3, so it's on the right border but (3,4) is banned.

Actually, (3,4) is on the border (right column), so it's banned.

So valid border positions: (1,2), (1,3), (4,2), (4,3), (2,1), (2,4)

But we can only place one chip because if we place two chips, they might meet or swap.

The answer is 1, which suggests we can place at most one chip.

## Conclusion

After analyzing the constraints, it seems that:
1. We can't place more than one chip per "vertical column" or "horizontal row" because they'll meet in the middle
2. But more importantly, any two chips moving toward each other will swap paths

The key insight from known similar problems is that we can place at most one chip per "parallel path" - specifically, we can have at most one chip in the top/bottom moving vertically, and at most one in the left/right moving horizontally.

However, the swapping condition is critical. If two chips are moving in opposite directions, they swap.

Actually, looking more carefully: if we place chips on non-opposite edges, they might not swap. But if we place chips on edge pairs that are opposite, they will swap.

But the problem states "at least once two chips swapped" - so any pair of chips that move toward each other will swap.

Therefore, we can only place chips that don't cross paths. 

The safest approach is to place chips only on the same side (e.g., all on top row) or on non-opposite sides.

Actually, let's think about it differently: we can place chips on the same "edge type" without swapping. But if we place chips on opposite edges, they will eventually swap.

So we can place multiple chips on the same edge (top, bottom, left, right) as long as they don't collide and don't reach banned cells.

But chips on the same edge will move in the same direction and won't meet each other or swap.

For example: two chips on the top row will both move down. They will never be on the same cell, and they won't swap.

Wait, but the problem says "two chips were on the same cell" - this would happen if they meet. But if they're on different positions on the top row, they move down but don't meet until they're at the same row.

Actually, they move down at the same time - so if they start at (1,2) and (1,3), after 1 minute: (2,2) and (2,3), etc. They will never be on the same cell.

So multiple chips on the same edge don't cause issues.

The only issues are:
1. Reaching banned cells
2. Two chips on same cell (impossible with different starting positions)
3. Swapping (which happens when chips from opposite edges cross paths)

So we can place multiple chips on the same edge.

But what about \(k\) chips on the same edge? Will they ever be on the same cell? No, because they start at different columns and move down together.

Will they swap? No, because they're moving in the same direction.

The only risk is reaching banned cells.

Therefore, the answer is the maximum number of valid border cells we can pick such that no chip reaches a banned cell.

However, we might be able to place more than one chip on one edge, but we need to check if any of the paths go through banned cells.

For each chip on edge, its path is determined:
- Top row chip at (1, j): path is (1,j) → (2,j) → (3,j) → ... → (n,j)
- Bottom row chip at (n, j): path is (n,j) → (n-1,j) → ... → (1,j)
- Left column chip at (i, 1): path is (i,1) → (i,2) → (i,3) → ... → (i,n)
- Right column chip at (i, n): path is (i,n) → (i,n-1) → ... → (i,1)

A chip at position (x,y) can move to (x',y') after t minutes if |x-x'| + |y-y'| = t and t ≤ n-1.

For a chip on top row (1,j), after t minutes, it's at (1+t, j). This is valid for t = 0 to n-1.

So for a chip at (1,j), it reaches (t+1, j) at minute t.

This chip is valid if (t+1, j) is not banned for any t from 0 to n-1.

Similarly for other edge positions.

The maximum number of chips we can place is the maximum subset of border non-corner cells such that for each chip, its entire path doesn't go through banned cells.

But this is complex. Let me look for a simpler pattern.

Known similar problems suggest that the answer is often 1 or 0, because of the swapping constraint.

Actually, re-reading the problem: "at least once two chips swapped". This happens when two chips cross paths.

If we place chips on the same edge, they don't swap.

If we place chips on opposite edges, they swap.

Therefore, we should avoid placing chips on opposite edges.

But we can place multiple chips on the same edge.

So we should consider each edge separately and count how many valid positions we have on each edge.

Then take the maximum number of chips across edges.

But we need to ensure that for a chip on a given edge, none of its path cells are banned.

For a chip on top row at (1,j), it visits cells (1,j), (2,j), (3,j), ..., (n,j). All these must not be banned.

Similarly for other edges.

So the algorithm:

1. Read input
2. Create a set of banned cells
3. For each edge, find valid starting positions (border non-corner)
4. For each valid position, check if its path (all cells it visits) contains any banned cell
5. For each edge, count valid positions
6. The answer is the maximum count across edges

```python
n, m = map(int, input().split())

banned = set()
for _ in range(m):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    banned.add((x, y))

max_chips = 0

# Top row (row 1): columns 2 to n-1
for j in range(2, n):
    # Check path: (1,j), (2,j), ..., (n,j)
    valid = True
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if (i, j) in banned:
            valid = False
            break
    if valid:
        max_chips = max(max_chips, 1)  # We can place at least one chip here

# Bottom row (row n): columns 2 to n-1
for j in range(2, n):
    # Check path: (n,j), (n-1,j), ..., (1,j)
    valid = True
    for i in range(n, 0, -1):
        if (i, j) in banned:
            valid = False
            break
    if valid:
        max_chips = max(max_chips, 1)

# Left column (column 1): rows 2 to n-1
for i in range(2, n):
    # Check path: (i,1), (i,2), ..., (i,n)
    valid = True
    for j in range(1, n + 1):
        if (i, j) in banned:
            valid = False
            break
    if valid:
        max_chips = max(max_chips, 1)

        if (i, j) in banned:
            valid = False
            break
    if valid:
        max_chips = max(max_chips, 1)

print(max_chips)
```

Wait, I think I made a mistake. This counts 1 chip per edge, but actually we can place multiple chips on the same edge (as long as they don't cross paths).

For multiple chips on the same edge, they don't swap or collide, so we should count all valid positions on each edge.

```python
n, m = map(int, input().split())

banned = set()
for _ in range(m):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    banned.add((x, y))

max_chips = 0

# Top row (row 1): columns 2 to n-1
valid_top = 0
for j in range(2, n):
    # Check path: (1,j), (2,j), ..., (n,j)
    valid = True
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if (i, j) in banned:
            valid = False
            break
    if valid:
        valid_top += 1

max_chips = max(max_chips, valid_top)

# Bottom row (row n): columns 2 to n-1
valid_bottom = 0
for j in range(2, n):
    # Check path: (n,j), (n-1,j), ..., (1,j)
    valid = True
    for i in range(n, 0, -1):
        if (i, j) in banned:
            valid = False
            break
    if valid:
        valid_bottom += 1

max_chips = max(max_chips, valid_bottom)

# Left column (column 1): rows 2 to n-1
valid_left = 0
for i in range(2, n):
    # Check path: (i,1), (i,2), ..., (i,n)
    valid = True
    for j in range(1, n + 1):
        if (i, j) in banned:
            valid = False
            break
    if valid:
        valid_left += 1

max_chips = max(max_chips, valid_left)

# Right column (column n): rows 2 to n-1
valid_right = 0
for i in range(2, n):
    # Check path: (i,n), (i,n-1), ..., (i,1)
    valid = True
    for j in range(n, 0, -1):
        if (i, j) in banned:
            valid = False
            break
    if valid:
        valid_right += 1

max_chips = max(max_chips, valid_right)

print(max_chips)
```

This should be correct. Let me verify with examples:

Example 1: n=3, m=1, banned at (2,2)
- Top row (j=2): path (1,2),(2,2),(3,2) - contains (2,2) → invalid
- Bottom row (j=2): path (3,2),(2,2),(1,2) - contains (2,2) → invalid
- Left column (i=2): path (2,1),(2,2),(2,3) - contains (2,2) → invalid
- Right column (i=2): path (2,3),(2,2),(2,1) - contains (2,2) → invalid
- All valid counts = 0 → output 0 ✓

Example 2: n=3, m=0
- All paths are valid
- Top: j=2 only → 1
- Bottom: j=2 only → 1  
- Left: i=2 only → 1
- Right: i=2 only → 1
- max = 1 ✓

Example 3: n=4, banned at (3,1),(3,2),(3,3)

- Left column (i=2,3):

  - i=3: path (3,1),(3,2),(3,3),(3,4) → (3,1),(3,2),(3,3) banned → invalid
  - valid_left = 1
- Right column (i=2,3):
  - i=2: path (2,4),(2,3),(2,2),(2,1) → all non-banned → valid
  - i=3: path (3,4),(3,3),(3,2),(3,1) → (3,3),(3,2),(3,1) banned → invalid
  - valid_right = 1
- max = 1 ✓

Perfect! This matches all examples.

```python
            break
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an n×n grid with m banned cells
2. Chips must start on border cells (but not corners)
3. Each chip moves every minute to an adjacent cell (up, down, left, right)
4. Gerald loses if:
   - Any chip lands on a banned cell
   - Two chips are on the same cell
   - Two chips swap positions in one minute (swap means they exchange positions)
5. We want to maximize the number of chips we can place

## Key Insights

1. **Border cells (non-corner)**: For an n×n grid, border cells are:
   - Top row: (1, 2) to (1, n-1)
   - Bottom row: (n, 2) to (n, n-1) 
   - Left column: (2, 1) to (n-1, 1)
   - Right column: (2, n) to (n-1, n)

2. **Movement pattern**: Each chip moves one cell per minute toward the opposite edge. After (n-1) minutes, it reaches the opposite edge.

3. **Swap condition**: Two chips swap if they start on cells that are symmetric with respect to the center, and they move toward opposite edges.

4. **Conflict analysis**: 
   - Chips cannot share the same cell at any time
   - Chips cannot swap positions

## Critical Observation

For two chips to not swap, they must not start at positions that are symmetric in a way that would cause them to exchange places during movement.

However, the key insight is that if we place chips on cells that are symmetric with respect to the center, they will eventually swap positions in the middle.

But there's a more fundamental constraint: **if we place k chips, they must be placed such that no two chips would occupy the same cell at any time, and no two chips would swap positions**.

Let me think about the center of the grid. If we place a chip on position (i,j), after t minutes it will be at position (i+t, j) or (i-t, j) or (i, j+t) or (i, j-t), depending on direction.

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Gerald plays the following game. He has a checkered field of size n × n cells, where m various cells are banned. Before the game, he has to put a few chips on some border (but not corner) board cells. Then for n - 1 minutes, Gerald every minute moves each chip into an adjacent cell. He moves each chip from its original edge to the opposite edge. Gerald loses in this game in each of the three cases:

  * At least one of the chips at least once fell to the banned cell. 
  * At least once two chips were on the same cell. 
  * At least once two chips swapped in a minute (for example, if you stand two chips on two opposite border cells of a row with even length, this situation happens in the middle of the row). 



In that case he loses and earns 0 points. When nothing like that happened, he wins and earns the number of points equal to the number of chips he managed to put on the board. Help Gerald earn the most points.

Input

The first line contains two space-separated integers n and m (2 ≤ n ≤ 1000, 0 ≤ m ≤ 105) — the size of the field and the number of banned cells. Next m lines each contain two space-separated integers. Specifically, the i-th of these lines contains numbers xi and yi (1 ≤ xi, yi ≤ n) — the coordinates of the i-th banned cell. All given cells are distinct.

Consider the field rows numbered from top to bottom from 1 to n, and the columns — from left to right from 1 to n.

Output

Print a single integer — the maximum points Gerald can earn in this game.

Examples

Input

3 1
2 2


Output

0


Input

3 0


Output

1


Input

4 3
3 1
3 2
3 3


Output

1

Note

In the first test the answer equals zero as we can't put chips into the corner cells.

In the second sample we can place one chip into either cell (1, 2), or cell (3, 2), or cell (2, 1), or cell (2, 3). We cannot place two chips.

In the third sample we can only place one chip into either cell (2, 1), or cell (2, 4).### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an n×n grid with m banned cells
2. Chips must be placed on border cells (but not corners)
3. Each chip moves toward the opposite edge each minute (so from edge to opposite edge)
4. Gerald loses if:
   - Any chip lands on a banned cell
   - Two chips occupy the same cell
   - Two chips swap positions (exchange places)

## Key Insights

1. **Border cells (not corners)**: These are edge cells excluding corners. For an n×n grid, border cells are:
   - Top row: (1, 2) to (1, n-1)
   - Bottom row: (n, 2) to (n, n-1)
   - Left column: (2, 1) to (n-1, 1)
   - Right column: (2, n) to (n-1, n)

2. **Movement pattern**: Each chip moves from its starting border position toward the opposite edge. For example:
   - A chip at (1, j) moves down to (n, j)
   - A chip at (n, j) moves up to (1, j)
   - A chip at (i, 1) moves right to (i, n)
   - A chip at (i, n) moves left to (i, 1)

3. **Swap condition**: Two chips swap positions when they meet exactly in the middle and pass through each other. This happens when:
   - Two chips are on opposite edges and meet in the middle
   - For example: chip at (1, j) moving down meets chip at (n, k) moving up

4. **Critical observation**: For two chips to avoid swapping, they must not meet at any point in time. This means their movement paths must not cross.

## Analysis of Movement Paths

For a chip starting at (1, j):
- At time t, it's at position (1+t, j) for t from 0 to n-1
- When t = n-1, it reaches (n, j)

For a chip starting at (n, k):
- At time t, it's at position (n-t, k) for t from 0 to n-1
- When t = n-1, it reaches (1, k)

They meet when: 1+t = n-t and j = k, which gives t = (n-1)/2.

For them to avoid swapping, they must not meet at any time, which means they can't be on the same column and positioned such that their paths cross.

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    banned = set()
    for _ in range(m):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        banned.add((x, y))
    
    # Check if any border cell (not corner) is valid
    valid_positions = []
    
    # Top row (row 1): columns 2 to n-1
    for j in range(2, n):
        # Chip at (1, j) moves down to (n, j)
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):  # t=1 to n-1, since at t=0 it's start
            row = 1 + t
            if (row, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((1, j))
    
    # Bottom row (row n): columns 2 to n-1
    for j in range(2, n):
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):
            row = n - t
            if (row, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((n, j))
    
    # Left column (column 1): rows 2 to n-1
    for i in range(2, n):
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):
            col = 1 + t
            if (i, col) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((i, 1))
    
    # Right column (column n): rows 2 to n-1
    for i in range(2, n):
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):
            col = n - t
            if (i, col) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((i, n))
    
    if valid_positions:
        print(1)
    else:
        print(0)

solve()
```

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    banned = set()
    for _ in range(m):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        banned.add((x, y))
    
    # Check if any border cell (not corner) is valid
    valid_positions = []
    
    # Top row (row 1): columns 2 to n-1
    for j in range(2, n):
        if (1, j) in banned:
            continue
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):  # t=1 to n-1, since at t=0 it's start
            row = 1 + t
            if (row, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((1, j))
    
    # Bottom row (row n): columns 2 to n-1
    for j in range(2, n):
        if (n, j) in banned:
            continue
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):
            row = n - t
            if (row, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((n, j))
    
    # Left column (column 1): rows 2 to n-1
    for i in range(2, n):
        if (i, 1) in banned:
            continue
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):
            col = 1 + t
            if (i, col) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((i, 1))

        valid = True

                valid = False
                break
```

View Extracted Code 
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    banned = set()
    for _ in range(m):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        banned.add((x, y))
    
    # Check if any border cell (not corner) is valid
    valid_positions = []
    
    # Top row (row 1): columns 2 to n-1
    for j in range(2, n):
        if (1, j) in banned:
            continue
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):  # t=1 to n-1, since at t=0 it's start
            row = 1 + t
            if (row, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((1, j))
    
    # Bottom row (row n): columns 2 to n-1
    for j in range(2, n):
        if (n, j) in banned:
            continue
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):
            row = n - t
            if (row, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((n, j))
    
    # Left column (column 1): rows 2 to n-1
    for i in range(2, n):
        if (i, 1) in banned:
            continue
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):
            col = 1 + t
            if (i, col) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((i, 1))
    
    # Right column (column n): rows 2 to n-1
    for i in range(2, n):
        if (i, n) in banned:
            continue
        valid = True
        for t in range(1, n):
            col = n - t
            if (i, col) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((i, n))
    
    if valid_positions:
        print(1)
    else:
        print(0)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Gerald plays the following game. He has a checkered field of size n × n cells, where m various cells are banned. Before the game, he has to put a few chips on some border (but not corner) board cells. Then for n - 1 minutes, Gerald every minute moves each chip into an adjacent cell. He moves each chip from its original edge to the opposite edge. Gerald loses in this game in each of the three cases:

  * At least one of the chips at least once fell to the banned cell. 
  * At least once two chips were on the same cell. 
  * At least once two chips swapped in a minute (for example, if you stand two chips on two opposite border cells of a row with even length, this situation happens in the middle of the row). 



In that case he loses and earns 0 points. When nothing like that happened, he wins and earns the number of points equal to the number of chips he managed to put on the board. Help Gerald earn the most points.

Input

The first line contains two space-separated integers n and m (2 ≤ n ≤ 1000, 0 ≤ m ≤ 105) — the size of the field and the number of banned cells. Next m lines each contain two space-separated integers. Specifically, the i-th of these lines contains numbers xi and yi (1 ≤ xi, yi ≤ n) — the coordinates of the i-th banned cell. All given cells are distinct.

Consider the field rows numbered from top to bottom from 1 to n, and the columns — from left to right from 1 to n.

Output

Print a single integer — the maximum points Gerald can earn in this game.

Examples

Input

3 1
2 2


Output

0


Input

3 0


Output

1


Input

4 3
3 1
3 2
3 3


Output

1

Note

In the first test the answer equals zero as we can't put chips into the corner cells.

In the second sample we can place one chip into either cell (1, 2), or cell (3, 2), or cell (2, 1), or cell (2, 3). We cannot place two chips.

In the third sample we can only place one chip into either cell (2, 1), or cell (2, 4).### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an n×n grid with m banned cells
2. We can place chips only on border cells that are NOT corners (so only edge cells excluding corners)
3. Each chip moves every minute toward the opposite edge
4. We lose if:
   - Any chip lands on a banned cell
   - Two chips are on the same cell at the same time
   - Two chips swap positions (they exchange places)

## Key Observations

1. **Border cells (non-corner)**: For an n×n grid, the non-corner edge cells are:
   - Top edge: (1,2), (1,3), ..., (1,n-1)
   - Bottom edge: (n,2), (n,3), ..., (n,n-1)
   - Left edge: (2,1), (3,1), ..., (n-1,1)
   - Right edge: (2,n), (3,n), ..., (n-1,n)

2. **Movement pattern**: Each chip moves from its starting position toward the opposite edge. For example:
   - A chip on top edge (1,j) moves down to bottom edge (n,j)
   - A chip on bottom edge (n,j) moves up to top edge (1,j)
   - A chip on left edge (i,1) moves right to right edge (i,n)
   - A chip on right edge (i,n) moves left to left edge (i,1)

3. **Swapping condition**: Two chips swap when they are on opposite edges of the same row or column and meet in the middle. This happens if we have:
   - Two chips on opposite edges of the same row (one on left, one on right)
   - Two chips on opposite edges of the same column (one on top, one on bottom)

4. **Collision condition**: Two chips can't be on the same cell at any time.

## Analysis of Swapping and Collision

For chips on the same row:
- A chip from left edge (i,1) moves to (i,n)
- A chip from right edge (i,n) moves to (i,1)
- They swap at time t where they meet at position (i, (1+t) and (i, n-t))

For chips on the same column:
- A chip from top edge (1,j) moves to (n,j)
- A chip from bottom edge (n,j) moves to (1,j)
- They swap at the midpoint

The key insight is that if we place chips on the same row or column, they will eventually swap positions, which causes a loss.

## Solution Strategy

The safe strategy is to place chips such that:
1. Chips don't share the same row or column
2. No chip lands on a banned cell during its journey

Since swapping is not allowed, we can only have at most one chip per row and per column.

```python
n, m = map(int, input().split())
banned = set()
for _ in range(m):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    banned.add((x, y))

# Find all valid non-corner border positions
valid_positions = []

# Top edge (excluding corners)
for j in range(2, n):
    valid_positions.append((1, j))

# Bottom edge (excluding corners)
for j in range(2, n):
    valid_positions.append((n, j))

# Left edge (excluding corners)
for i in range(2, n):
    valid_positions.append((i, 1))

# Right edge (excluding corners)
for i in range(2, n):
    valid_positions.append((i, n))

# Check if a position is valid (no banned cell on its path)
def is_valid_position(pos):
    r, c = pos
    if r == 1:  # top edge
        # moves down to (n, c)
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            if (i, c) in banned:
                return False
    elif r == n:  # bottom edge
        # moves up to (1, c)
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            if (i, c) in banned:
                return False
    elif c == 1:  # left edge
        # moves right to (r, n)
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if (r, j) in banned:
                return False
    elif c == n:  # right edge
        # moves left to (r, 1)
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if (r, j) in banned:
                return False
    return True

# Filter valid positions
valid_positions = [pos for pos in valid_positions if is_valid_position(pos)]

# Now we need to select maximum number of positions such that no two share row or column
# This is equivalent to finding maximum matching in bipartite graph
# We can use greedy or check if any row/column has multiple valid positions

# Since the constraint is that no two chips can be in same row or column,
# we can assign at most one chip per row and per column.

# Build list of (row, col) for valid positions
positions = []
for r, c in valid_positions:
    positions.append((r, c))

# We want to select maximum subset with no two in same row or column
# This is maximum independent set in bipartite graph
# We can use greedy algorithm or simple loop

# Since n is small (<=1000), we can use a matching approach
# But note: positions are only on edges, so we have specific constraints

# Simple greedy: try to assign positions to rows and columns
# We'll use bipartite matching

from collections import defaultdict

# Build graph: row -> list of valid columns
row_to_cols = defaultdict(list)
col_to_rows = defaultdict(list)

for r, c in positions:
    row_to_cols[r].append(c)
    col_to_rows[c].append(r)

# Maximum matching in bipartite graph
# We'll use augmenting path algorithm (Hungarian)

def max_matching(graph, n_rows, n_cols):
    # graph[i] = list of columns connected to row i
    match_col = [-1] * (n_cols + 1)  # match_col[j] = row matched to column j
    match_row = [-1] * (n_rows + 1)  # match_row[i] = column matched to row i
    
    def dfs(u, visited):
        for v in graph[u]:
            if visited[v]:
                continue
            visited[v] = True
            if match_col[v] == -1 or dfs(match_col[v], visited):
                match_col[v] = u
                match_row[u] = v
                return True
        return False
    
    matching = 0
    for i in range(1, n_rows + 1):
        visited = [False] * (n_cols + 1)
        if dfs(i, visited):
            matching += 1
    
    return matching

# But note: our rows and columns might be limited to the ones in the valid positions
# We have rows and columns that appear in the valid positions
rows_in_use = set()
cols_in_use = set()
for r, c in positions:
    rows_in_use.add(r)
    cols_in_use.add(c)

# Create graph for these rows and columns
graph = {}
for r in rows_in_use:
    graph[r] = []
    for c in col_to_rows[c]:
        # Actually need to build properly
        pass

# Let me rebuild
row_to_cols_clean = {}
for r in rows_in_use:
    row_to_cols_clean[r] = []
    for c in col_to_rows:
        if c in col_to_rows and r in row_to_cols.get(r, []):
            # This is messy
            pass

# Actually, simpler: just find maximum matching in the bipartite graph
# We'll build the graph properly

# reinitialize
graph = {}
for r, c in positions:
    if r not in graph:
        graph[r] = []
    graph[r].append(c)

# Now find maximum matching
match_col = [-1] * (n + 1)  # 1-indexed
match_row = [-1] * (n + 1)

def dfs(u, visited):
    for v in graph.get(u, []):
        if visited[v]:
            continue
        visited[v] = True
        if match_col[v] == -1 or dfs(match_col[v], visited):
            match_col[v] = u
            match_row[u] = v
            return True
    return False

matching = 0
for i in range(1, n + 1):
    if i in graph:  # only consider rows that have valid positions
        visited = [False] * (n + 1)
        if dfs(i, visited):
            matching += 1

print(matching)
```

```python

        valid = True
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            if (i, c) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((r, c))
    elif r == n:  # bottom edge moves up
        valid = True
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            if (i, c) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((r, c))
    elif c == 1:  # left edge moves right
        valid = True
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if (r, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((r, c))
    else:  # right edge moves left
        valid = True
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if (r, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((r, c))

# Build bipartite graph: row -> column
row_to_cols = {}
for r, c in valid_positions:
    if r not in row_to_cols:
        row_to_cols[r] = []
    row_to_cols[r].append(c)

# Find maximum matching in bipartite graph
match_col = [-1] * (n + 1)  # match_col[j] = row matched to column j

def can_match(row, visited):
    for col in row_to_cols.get(row, []):
        if visited[col]:
            continue
        visited[col] = True
        if match_col[col] == -1 or can_match(match_col[col], visited):
            match_col[col] = row
            return True
    return False

matching = 0
for row in range(1, n + 1):
    if row in row_to_cols:
        visited = [False] * (n + 1)
        if can_match(row, visited):
            matching += 1

print(matching)
```

View Extracted Code 
n, m = map(int, input().split())
banned = set()
for _ in range(m):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    banned.add((x, y))

# Find all non-corner border positions
positions = []

# Top edge
for j in range(2, n):
    positions.append((1, j))

# Bottom edge
for j in range(2, n):
    positions.append((n, j))

# Left edge
for i in range(2, n):
    positions.append((i, 1))

# Right edge
for i in range(2, n):
    positions.append((i, n))

# Filter valid positions: path should not contain any banned cell
valid_positions = []
for r, c in positions:
    if r == 1:  # top edge moves down
        valid = True
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            if (i, c) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((r, c))
    elif r == n:  # bottom edge moves up
        valid = True
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            if (i, c) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((r, c))
    elif c == 1:  # left edge moves right
        valid = True
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if (r, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((r, c))
    else:  # right edge moves left
        valid = True
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if (r, j) in banned:
                valid = False
                break
        if valid:
            valid_positions.append((r, c))

# Build bipartite graph: row -> column
row_to_cols = {}
for r, c in valid_positions:
    if r not in row_to_cols:
        row_to_cols[r] = []
    row_to_cols[r].append(c)

# Find maximum matching in bipartite graph
match_col = [-1] * (n + 1)  # match_col[j] = row matched to column j

def can_match(row, visited):
    for col in row_to_cols.get(row, []):
        if visited[col]:
            continue
        visited[col] = True
        if match_col[col] == -1 or can_match(match_col[col], visited):
            match_col[col] = row
            return True
    return False

matching = 0
for row in range(1, n + 1):
    if row in row_to_cols:
        visited = [False] * (n + 1)
        if can_match(row, visited):
            matching += 1

print(matching)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Gerald plays the following game. He has a checkered field of size n × n cells, where m various cells are banned. Before the game, he has to put a few chips on some border (but not corner) board cells. Then for n - 1 minutes, Gerald every minute moves each chip into an adjacent cell. He moves each chip from its original edge to the opposite edge. Gerald loses in this game in each of the three cases:

  * At least one of the chips at least once fell to the banned cell. 
  * At least once two chips were on the same cell. 
  * At least once two chips swapped in a minute (for example, if you stand two chips on two opposite border cells of a row with even length, this situation happens in the middle of the row). 



In that case he loses and earns 0 points. When nothing like that happened, he wins and earns the number of points equal to the number of chips he managed to put on the board. Help Gerald earn the most points.

Input

The first line contains two space-separated integers n and m (2 ≤ n ≤ 1000, 0 ≤ m ≤ 105) — the size of the field and the number of banned cells. Next m lines each contain two space-separated integers. Specifically, the i-th of these lines contains numbers xi and yi (1 ≤ xi, yi ≤ n) — the coordinates of the i-th banned cell. All given cells are distinct.

Consider the field rows numbered from top to bottom from 1 to n, and the columns — from left to right from 1 to n.

Output

Print a single integer — the maximum points Gerald can earn in this game.

Examples

Input

3 1
2 2


Output

0


Input

3 0


Output

1


Input

4 3
3 1
3 2
3 3


Output

1

In the first test the answer equals zero as we can't put chips into the corner cells.

In the second sample we can place one chip into either cell (1, 2), or cell (3, 2), or cell (2, 1), or cell (2, 3). We cannot place two chips.

In the third sample we can only place one chip into either cell (2, 1), or cell (2, 4).### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an n×n grid with m banned cells
2. We can place chips only on border cells that are NOT corners (so only edge cells that are not at corners)
3. Each chip moves every minute to an adjacent cell (up/down/left/right)
4. We lose if:
   - Any chip lands on a banned cell
   - Two chips are on the same cell at the same time
   - Two chips swap positions (they meet and then pass through each other)

## Key Insights

1. **Border cells (non-corners)**: For an n×n grid, the border non-corner cells are:
   - Top row: positions (1,2), (1,3), ..., (1,n-1)
   - Bottom row: positions (n,2), (n,3), ..., (n,n-1) 
   - Left column: positions (2,1), (3,1), ..., (n-1,1)
   - Right column: positions (2,n), (3,n), ..., (n-1,n)

2. **Movement pattern**: Each chip starts at a border cell and moves toward the opposite border. For example:
   - A chip at (1,2) will move to (2,2), then (3,2), etc.
   - A chip at (2,1) will move to (3,1), then (4,1), etc.

3. **Swapping condition**: Two chips swap if they start on opposite sides of the grid and meet in the middle. This happens when chips move toward each other and pass through each other.

4. **Collision condition**: Two chips occupy the same cell at the same time.

## Analysis of Constraints

The key insight is that we need to avoid:
- Chips landing on banned cells
- Chips occupying the same cell
- Chips swapping

For chips to avoid swapping, they need to move in the same direction (all left-to-right, or all right-to-left, etc.). But if we place two chips on opposite sides of the grid, they'll meet and swap at some point.

Therefore, we can only place chips that won't meet each other, and won't hit banned cells.

So algorithm:

1. Read n, m and banned cells
2. Create a set of banned cells
3. Initialize count = 0
4. For each top row non-corner cell (1,c) where 2<=c<=n-1:
   - Check if any cell (i,c) for i from 2 to n is banned
   - If not, increment count
5. For each bottom row non-corner cell (n,c) where 2<=c<=n-1:
   - Check if any cell (i,c) for i from 1 to n-1 is banned
   - If not, increment count
6. For each left column non-corner cell (r,1) where 2<=r<=n-1:
   - Check if any cell (r,j) for j from 2 to n is banned
   - If not, increment count

For example 1, no valid position (because (2,2) is banned), so answer 0.

Therefore, the solution is:

1. Check if any border non-corner cell has a valid path (no banned cells in its path)
2. If yes, output 1
3. If no, output 0

Let me verify with the examples.

Example 1: n=3, banned (2,2)
- (1,2): path (2,2) - banned → invalid
- (3,2): path (2,2) - banned → invalid
- (2,1): path (2,2) - banned → invalid
- (2,3): path (2,2) - banned → invalid
- So no valid position → output 0 ✓

Example 2: n=3, m=0
- All positions have valid paths → at least one valid → output 1 ✓

Example 3: n=4, banned (3,1),(3,2),(3,3)
- (2,1): path (2,2),(2,3),(2,4) - not banned → valid
- (2,4): path (2,3),(2,2),(2,1) - not banned → valid
- So at least one valid → output 1 ✓

Therefore, the answer is 1 if at least one valid position exists, 0 otherwise.

```python
n, m = map(int, input().split())

banned = set()
for _ in range(m):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    banned.add((x, y))

# Check if any border non-corner cell has a valid path
valid_position = False

# Top row: (1, c) for c from 2 to n-1
for c in range(2, n):
    # Path: (2,c), (3,c), ..., (n,c)
    for row in range(2, n+1):
        if (row, c) in banned:
            break
    else:
        # No banned cell in path
        valid_position = True
        break

if valid_position:
    # Check bottom row if not found
    for c in range(2, n):
        # Path: (n-1,c), (n-2,c), ..., (1,c)
        for row in range(n-1, 0, -1):
            if (row, c) in banned:
                break
        else:
            valid_position = True
            break

if not valid_position:
    # Check left column: (r,1) for r from 2 to n-1
    for r in range(2, n):
        # Path: (r,2), (r,3), ..., (r,n)
        for col in range(2, n+1):
            if (r, col) in banned:
                break
        else:
            valid_position = True
            break

if not valid_position:
    # Check right column: (r,n) for r from 2 to n-1
    for r in range(2, n):
        # Path: (r,n-1), (r,n-2), ..., (r,1)
        for col in range(n-1, 0, -1):
            if (r, col) in banned:
                break
        else:
            valid_position = True
            break

# If at least one valid position exists, max points is 1, else 0
print(1 if valid_position else 0)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Gerald plays the following game. He has a checkered field of size n × n cells, where m various cells are banned. Before the game, he has to put a few chips on some border (but not corner) board cells. Then for n - 1 minutes, Gerald every minute moves each chip into an adjacent cell. He moves each chip from its original edge to the opposite edge. Gerald loses in this game in each of the three cases:

  * At least one of the chips at least once fell to the banned cell. 
  * At least once two chips were on the same cell. 
  * At least once two chips swapped in a minute (for example, if you stand two chips on two opposite border cells of a row with even length, this situation happens in the middle of the row). 



In that case he loses and earns 0 points. When nothing like that happened, he wins and earns the number of points equal to the number of chips he managed to put on the board. Help Gerald earn the most points.

Input

The first line contains two space-separated integers n and m (2 ≤ n ≤ 1000, 0 ≤ m ≤ 105) — the size of the field and the number of banned cells. Next m lines each contain two space-separated integers. Specifically, the i-th of these lines contains numbers xi and yi (1 ≤ xi, yi ≤ n) — the coordinates of the i-th banned cell. All given cells are distinct.

Consider the field rows numbered from top to bottom from 1 to n, and the columns — from left to right from 1 to n.

Output

Print a single integer — the maximum points Gerald can earn in this game.

Examples

Input

3 1
2 2


Output

0


Input

3 0


Output

1


Input

4 3
3 1
3 2
3 3


Output

1

Note

In the first test the answer equals zero as we can't put chips into the corner cells.

In the second sample we can place one chip into either cell (1, 2), or cell (3, 2), or cell (2, 1), or cell (2, 3). We cannot place two chips.

In the third sample we can only place one chip into either cell (2, 1), or cell (2, 4).### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an n×n grid with m banned cells
2. We can only place chips on border cells that are NOT corners (so only edges but not corners)
3. Each chip moves toward the opposite edge over n-1 minutes
4. We lose if:
   - Any chip lands on a banned cell
   - Two chips occupy the same cell at the same time
   - Two chips swap positions (they exchange places)

## Key Insights

1. **Border cells (non-corners)**: For an n×n grid, the non-corner border cells are:
   - Top row: (1, 2) to (1, n-1)
   - Bottom row: (n, 2) to (n, n-1)  
   - Left column: (2, 1) to (n-1, 1)
   - Right column: (2, n) to (n-1, n)

2. **Chip movement**: A chip at position (r, c) moves toward the opposite edge. For example:
   - Top row chip (1, c) moves down to (n, c)
   - Bottom row chip (n, c) moves up to (1, c)
   - Left column chip (r, 1) moves right to (r, n)
   - Right column chip (r, n) moves left to (r, 1)

3. **Swapping condition**: Two chips swap when they meet at the center of the grid. This happens when:
   - Two chips are on opposite edges of the same row (top and bottom) and meet in the middle
   - Two chips are on opposite edges of the same column (left and right) and meet in the middle

4. **Collision conditions**: Two chips can collide if they reach the same cell at the same time.

5. **Key observation**: Since each chip moves exactly n-1 steps, and they move in straight lines, chips on the same row (top/bottom) will meet exactly at the middle of that row if n is odd, and they will meet at even positions if n is even. Similarly for columns.

## Strategy Analysis

For two chips to not swap and not collide:
- They cannot be on the same row (top/bottom) if they would meet in the middle
- They cannot be on the same column (left/right) if they would meet in the middle

In fact, if we have two chips on the same row (top/bottom), they will meet in the middle when n is odd, or at specific points when n is even. Since the problem says "two chips swap" happens when they exchange places, this is a critical constraint.

The problem states that chips "swap" when they exchange places. This means if we have two chips on two opposite border cells of a row with even length, they will swap in the middle.

For a row of length n:
- If n is even, chips from (1, c) and (n, c) will meet at positions (n//2+1, c) and (n//2, c) - they don't exactly swap but meet
- If n is odd, they meet at (n//2 + 1, c) (center)

But the problem specifically says "two chips swapped" - this likely means when they exchange positions. This happens when they start on opposite edges and meet in the middle.

For chips on the same row (top and bottom) or same column (left and right), they will meet at the center or halfway point. When they meet, if they were on opposite edges, they effectively "swap" positions.

```python
n, m = map(int, input().split())
banned = set()
for _ in range(m):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    banned.add((x, y))

# Check if any non-corner border cell can be used
# Non-corner border cells are:
# Top row: (1,2) to (1,n-1)
# Bottom row: (n,2) to (n,n-1)
# Left column: (2,1) to (n-1,1)
# Right column: (2,n) to (n-1,n)

can_place = False

# Check top row non-corners
for c in range(2, n):
    if (1, c) not in banned:
        can_place = True
        break

# Check bottom row non-corners  
for c in range(2, n):
    if (n, c) not in banned:
        can_place = True
        break

# Check left column non-corners
for r in range(2, n):
    if (r, 1) not in banned:
        can_place = True
        break

# Check right column non-corners
for r in range(2, n):
```

```python
n, m = map(int, input().split())
banned = set()
for _ in range(m):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    banned.add((x, y))

# Check if any non-corner border cell can be used
# We need to check the entire path of the chip
valid = False

# Check top row non-corners: (1, c) for c in [2, n-1]
for c in range(2, n):
    # Path: (1, c) -> (2, c) -> ... -> (n, c)
    path_valid = True
    for r in range(1, n):
        if (r+1, c) in banned:  # (r+1, c) is the next cell
            path_valid = False
            break
    if path_valid:
        valid = True
        break

# Check bottom row non-corners: (n, c) for c in [2, n-1]
for c in range(2, n):
    # Path: (n, c) -> (n-1, c) -> ... -> (1, c)
    path_valid = True
    for r in range(n-1, 0, -1):
        if (r, c) in banned:  # (r, c) is the next cell when moving up
            path_valid = False
            break
    if path_valid:
        valid = True
        break

# Check left column non-corners: (r, 1) for r in [2, n-1]
for r in range(2, n):
    # Path: (r, 1) -> (r, 2) -> ... -> (r, n)
    path_valid = True
    for c in range(1, n):
        if (r, c+1) in banned:  # (r, c+1) is the next cell
            path_valid = False
            break
    if path_valid:
        valid = True
        break

# Check right column non-corners: (r, n) for r in [2, n-1]
for r in range(2, n):
    # Path: (r, n) -> (r, n-1) -> ... -> (r, 1)
    path_valid = True
    for c in range(n-1, 0, -1):
        if (r, c) in banned:  # (r, c) is the next cell when moving left
            path_valid = False
            break
    if path_valid:
        valid = True
        break

print(1 if valid else 0)
```

```python
n, m = map(int, input().split())
banned = set()
for _ in range(m):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    banned.add((x, y))

valid = False

# Check top row non-corners: (1, c) for c in [2, n-1]
for c in range(2, n):
    # Check if any intermediate cell is banned
    path_banned = False
    for r in range(2, n+1):  # r goes from 2 to n
        if (r, c) in banned:
            path_banned = True
            break
    if not path_banned:
        valid = True
        break

# Check bottom row non-corners: (n, c) for c in [2, n-1]
for c in range(2, n):
    path_banned = False
    for r in range(n-1, 0, -1):  # r from n-1 down to 1
        if (r, c) in banned:
            path_banned = True
            break
    if not path_banned:
        valid = True
        break

# Check left column non-corners: (r, 1) for r in [2, n-1]
for r in range(2, n):
    path_banned = False
    for c in range(2, n+1):  # c from 2 to n
        if (r, c) in banned:
            path_banned = True
            break
    if not path_banned:
        valid = True
        break

# Check right column non-corners: (r, n) for r in [2, n-1]
for r in range(2, n):
    path_banned = False
    for c in range(n-1, 0, -1):  # c from n-1 down to 1
        if (r, c) in banned:
            path_banned = True
            break
    if not path_banned:
        valid = True
        break

print(1 if valid else 0)
```
